009202 | Note 1. Organization, Operations and Basis of Presentation
009203 | In October 2017, Honeywell International Inc. (“Honeywell” or the “Parent”) announced its plan to spin-off its Homes and ADI Global Distribution business into a stand-alone publicly-traded company.
009204 | The
009205 | Homes and ADI Global Distribution business (“Homes”, “Homes Business”, the “Business”, the “Company”, “we” or “our”) of Honeywell is a leading global provider of products, software,
009206 | solutions and technologies that help owners of homes stay connected and in control of their comfort, security and energy use. We are a leader in the home heating, ventilation and air conditioning controls and security markets, and a leading global
009207 | distributor of security and fire protection products.
009208 | These unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements were derived from the
009209 | consolidated financial statements and accounting records of Honeywell. These unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements reflect the combined historical results of operations, financial position and cash flows of Homes as they were historically
009210 | managed in conformity with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America (“U.S. GAAP”).
009211 | The Combined
009212 | Interim Financial Statements are unaudited; however, in the opinion of management, they contain all the adjustments (consisting of those of a normal recurring nature) considered necessary to state fairly the financial position, results of operations
009213 | and cash flows for the periods presented in conformity with U.S. GAAP applicable to interim periods. The unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements should be read in conjunction with the audited Combined Financial Statements of the Homes
009214 | business included herein. The results of operations for the three and six months ended June 30, 2018 and the cash flows for the six months ended June 30, 2018 should not necessarily be taken as indicative of the entire year.
009215 | All intracompany transactions have been eliminated. As described in Note 3. Related Party Transactions with Honeywell, all significant
009216 | transactions between the Business and Honeywell have been included in these unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements and are expected to be settled for cash prior to the spin-off. These transactions
009217 | which are expected to be settled for cash prior to the spin-off are reflected in the Combined Interim Balance Sheets as Due from related parties, current or Due to related parties, current. In the Combined
009218 | Interim Statements of Cash Flows, the cash flows related to related party notes receivables presented in the Combined Interim Balance Sheets in Due from related parties, current are reflected as investing activities since these balances represent
009219 | amounts loaned to Parent. The cash flows related to related party notes payables presented in the Combined Interim Balance Sheets in Due to related parties, current are reflected as financing activities since these balances represent amounts
009220 | financed by Parent.
009221 | Honeywell uses a centralized approach to cash management and financing of its operations. The majority of the
009222 | Business’s cash is transferred to Honeywell daily and Honeywell funds the Business’s operating and investing activities as needed. This arrangement is not reflective of the manner in which the Business would have been able to finance its
009223 | operations had it been a stand-alone business separate from Honeywell during the periods presented. Cash transfers to and from Honeywell’s cash management accounts are reflected in the Combined Interim Balance Sheet as Due to and Due from
009224 | related parties, current and in the Combined Interim Statements of Cash Flows as net financing activities.
009225 | The unaudited Combined Interim
009226 | Financial Statements include certain assets and liabilities that have historically been held at the Honeywell corporate level but are specifically identifiable or otherwise attributable to Homes. The cash and cash equivalents held by Honeywell at
009227 | the corporate level are not specifically
009228 | F-37
009229 | Table of Contents
009230 | HOMES BUSINESS OF HONEYWELL INTERNATIONAL INC.
009231 | NOTES TO COMBINED INTERIM FINANCIAL STATEMENTS (Continued)
009232 | (Unaudited)
009233 | identifiable to Homes and therefore were not attributed for any of the periods presented. Honeywell third-party debt and the related interest expense have not been allocated for any of the
009234 | periods presented as Honeywell’s borrowings were not directly attributable to Homes.
009235 | Honeywell provides certain services, such as
009236 | legal, accounting, information technology, human resources and other infrastructure support, on behalf of the Business. The cost of these services has been allocated to the Business on the basis of the proportion of revenues. The Business and
009237 | Honeywell consider these allocations to be a reasonable reflection of the benefits received by the Business. However, the financial information presented in these unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements may not reflect the combined financial
009238 | position, operating results and cash flows of the Business had the Business been a separate stand-alone entity during the periods presented. Actual costs that would have been incurred if the Business had been a stand-alone company would depend on
009239 | multiple factors, including organizational structure and strategic decisions made in various areas, including information technology and infrastructure. We consider the basis on which the expenses have been allocated to be a reasonable reflection of
009240 | the utilization of services provided to or the benefits received by the Business during the periods presented.
009241 | Note 2. Recent Accounting
009242 | Pronouncements
009243 | The accounting policies of the Business are set forth in Note 2. Summary of Significant Accounting Policies to the
009244 | Combined Financial Statements contained in the Business’s Combined Financial Statements for the year ended December 31, 2017. We include herein certain updates to those policies.
009245 | Sales Recognition—Product and service sales are recognized when or as we transfer control of the promised products or
009246 | services to our customer. Revenue is measured as the amount of consideration we expect to receive in exchange for transferring goods or providing services.
009247 | In the sale of products, the terms of a contract or the historical business practice can give rise to variable consideration due to, but not
009248 | limited to, discounts and bonuses. We estimate variable consideration at the most likely amount we will receive from customers and reduce revenues recognized accordingly. We include estimated amounts in the transaction price to the extent it is
009249 | probable that a significant reversal of cumulative revenue recognized will not occur when the uncertainty associated with the variable consideration is resolved. Our estimates of variable consideration and determination of whether to include
009250 | estimated amounts in the transaction price are based largely on an assessment of our anticipated performance and all information (historical, current and forecasted) that is reasonably available to us.
009251 | Recent Accounting Pronouncements—We consider the applicability and impact of all recent accounting
009252 | standards updates (“ASUs”) issued by the Financial Accounting Standards Board (FASB). ASUs not listed below were assessed and determined to be either not applicable or are expected to have an immaterial impact on the combined financial
009253 | position or results of operations.
009254 | In February 2016, the “FASB” issued guidance on accounting for leases which requires lessees
009255 | to recognize most leases on their balance sheets for the rights and obligations created by those leases. The guidance requires enhanced disclosures regarding the amount, timing, and uncertainty of cash flows arising from leases that will be
009256 | effective for interim and annual periods beginning after December 15, 2018, with early adoption permitted. We expect to adopt the requirements of the new standard effective January 1, 2019. The guidance requires the use of a modified
009257 | retrospective approach. We are currently evaluating our lease portfolio to assess the impact to the unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements as well as planning for adoption and implementation of this standard, which includes assessing the
009258 | impact on information systems and internal controls.

009264 | In August 2017, the FASB issued amendments to hedge accounting guidance. These amendments are
009265 | intended to better align a company’s risk management strategies and financial reporting for hedging relationships. Under the new guidance, more hedging strategies will be eligible for hedge accounting and the application of hedge accounting is
009266 | simplified. In addition, the new guidance amends presentation and disclosure requirements. The guidance is effective for fiscal years beginning after December 15, 2018 with early adoption permitted, including the interim periods within those
009267 | years. The guidance requires the use of a modified retrospective approach. We are currently evaluating the impact of the guidance on our unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements and whether we will early adopt this guidance.
009268 | In February 2018, the FASB issued guidance that allows for an entity to elect to reclassify the income tax effects on items within accumulated
009269 | other comprehensive income resulting from U.S. tax reform to retained earnings. The guidance is effective for fiscal years beginning after December 15, 2018 with early adoption permitted, including interim periods within those years. We are
009270 | currently evaluating the impact of this standard on our unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements and whether we will make the allowed election.
009271 | Note 3. Related Party Transactions with Honeywell
009272 | The unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements have been prepared on a stand-alone basis and are derived from the consolidated financial
009273 | statements and accounting records of Honeywell.
009274 | Honeywell provided certain services, such as legal, accounting, information technology,
009275 | human resources and other infrastructure support, on behalf of the Business. The cost of these services has been allocated to the Business on the basis of the proportion of revenues. The Business and Honeywell consider the allocations to be a
009276 | reasonable reflection of the benefits received by the Business. During the three months ended June 30, 2018 and 2017, Homes was allocated $69 million and $67 million, respectively, of general corporate expenses incurred by Honeywell.
009277 | During the six months ended June 30, 2018 and 2017, Homes was allocated $137 million and $137 million, respectively, of general corporate expenses incurred by Honeywell. Such amounts are included within Selling, general and
009278 | administrative expenses in the Combined Interim Statements of Operations. As certain expenses reflected in the unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements include allocations of corporate expenses from Honeywell, these statements could differ
009279 | from those that would have been prepared had Homes operated on a stand-alone basis.
009280 | All significant intercompany transactions between the
009281 | Business and Honeywell have been included in these unaudited Combined Interim Financial Statements and are considered to have been effectively settled or are expected to be settled for cash at the time the transaction is recorded. Sales to Honeywell

009286 | $105 million, respectively.

009551 | Note 5. Income Taxes
009552 | The effective tax rate decreased for the three months ended June 30, 2018, as compared to the three months ended June 30, 2017,
009553 | primarily due to internal restructuring of Resideo’s business in advance of its anticipated Spin-Off that resulted in an $18 million reduction in tax expense, increased tax benefits attributable to currency impacts for withholding taxes on
009554 | undistributed foreign earnings, adjustments to the provisional tax amount related to the Tax Act and decreased income before taxes.
009555 | The
009556 | effective tax rate decreased for the six months ended June 30, 2018, as compared to the six months ended June 30, 2017, primarily due to increased tax benefits attributable to internal restructuring of Resideo’s business in advance of
009557 | its anticipated Spin-Off that resulted in an $18 million reduction in tax expense, currency impacts for withholding taxes on undistributed foreign earnings and adjustments to the provisional tax amount related to the Tax Act.
009558 | The effective tax rate for the quarter and six months ended June 30, 2018 was lower than the U.S. federal statutory rate of 21% primarily
009559 | from tax benefits related to the internal restructuring of Resideo’s business in advance of its anticipated Spin-Off that resulted in an $18 million reduction in tax expense, currency impacts on withholding taxes on undistributed foreign
009560 | earnings and adjustments to the provisional tax amount related to the Tax Act.
009561 | The effective tax rate for the quarter and six months
009562 | ended in June 30, 2017 was higher than the U.S. federal statutory rate of 35% as a result of non-deductible expenses.
009563 | On December 22, 2017, the U.S. government enacted the Tax Act, which included changes to the taxation of foreign earnings by implementing
009564 | a dividend exemption system, expansion of the current anti-deferral rules, a minimum tax on low-taxed foreign earnings and new measures to deter base erosion. The Tax Act also included a permanent reduction in
009565 | the corporate tax rate to 21%, repeal of the corporate alternative minimum tax, expensing

009568 | HOMES BUSINESS OF HONEYWELL INTERNATIONAL INC.

009570 | (Unaudited)
009571 | of capital investment and limitation of the deduction for interest expense. Furthermore, as part of the transition to the new tax system, a one-time
009572 | transition tax was imposed on a U.S. shareholder’s historical undistributed earnings of foreign affiliates.
009573 | As described in our
009574 | Combined Financial Statements for the year ended December 31, 2017, we reasonably estimated certain effects of the Tax Act and, therefore, recorded provisional amounts, including the deemed repatriation transition tax and withholding taxes on
009575 | undistributed earnings. During the quarter, the Company recorded an adjustment to the provisional tax amount related to the deemed repatriation transition tax of $3 million. This adjustment results in a decrease to the effective tax rate for
009576 | the six months ended June 30, 2018 of 3.6%. The Company has not finalized the accounting for the tax effects of the Tax Act as we are continuing to gather additional information and expect to complete our accounting within the prescribed
009577 | measurement period.
009578 | On August 1, 2018, the Treasury department released proposed regulations regarding the deemed repatriation
009579 | transition tax. The Company is evaluating the impact of the proposed regulations as part of its overall analysis of the impacts of the Tax Act pursuant to SAB 118.

009622 | The timing of revenue recognition, billings and cash collections results in billed accounts receivable and unbilled receivables (contract
009623 | assets), reported in Accounts, notes and other receivables—net, and customer
009624 | F-43

009629 | advances and deposits (contract liabilities), reported in Accrued Liabilities, on the Combined Interim Balance Sheet. Contract assets arise when situations exist where the timing of cash
009630 | collected from customers that differs from the timing of revenue recognition. Contract assets are recognized when the revenue associated with the contract is recognized prior to billing and derecognized once invoiced in accordance with the terms of
009631 | the contract. Contract liabilities are recorded in scenarios where we enter into arrangements where customers are contractually obligated to remit cash payments in advance of us satisfying performance obligations and recognizing revenue. Contract
009632 | liabilities are derecognized when revenue is recognized, a milestone is met triggering the contractual right to bill, or the performance obligation is satisfied.

009656 | Performance Obligations
009657 | A performance obligation is a promise in a contract to transfer a distinct good or service to the customer, and is defined as the unit of
009658 | account. A contract’s transaction price is allocated to each distinct performance obligation and recognized as revenue when, or as, the performance obligation is satisfied. For product sales, typically each product sold to a customer represents
009659 | a distinct performance obligation.
009660 | The majority of our performance obligations are satisfied as of a point in time. Performance
009661 | obligations are supported by contracts with customers, providing a framework for the nature of the distinct goods, services or bundle of goods and services. The timing of satisfying the performance obligation is typically indicated by the terms of
009662 | the contract. All performance obligations are expected to be satisfied within one year.
009663 | The timing of satisfaction of our performance
009664 | obligations does not significantly vary from the typical timing of payment. For some contracts, we may be entitled to receive an advance payment.
009665 | We have applied the practical expedient to not disclose the value of remaining performance obligations for (i) contracts with an original
009666 | expected term of one year or less or (ii) contracts for which we recognize revenue in proportion to the amount we have the right to invoice for services performed.

009834 | Note 11. Commitments and Contingencies
009835 | Environmental Matters
009836 | We are
009837 | subject to various federal, state, local and foreign government requirements relating to the protection of the environment. We believe that, as a general matter, our policies, practices and procedures are properly designed to prevent unreasonable
009838 | risk of environmental damage and personal injury and that our handling, manufacture, use and disposal of hazardous substances are in accordance with environmental and safety laws and regulations. We have incurred remedial response and voluntary
009839 | cleanup costs for site contamination and are a party to lawsuits and claims associated with environmental and safety matters, including products containing hazardous substances. Additional lawsuits, claims and costs involving environmental matters
009840 | are likely to continue to arise in the future.
009841 | With respect to environmental matters involving site contamination, we continually conduct
009842 | studies, individually or jointly with other potentially responsible parties, to determine the feasibility of various remedial techniques. It is our policy to record appropriate liabilities for environmental matters when remedial efforts or damage
009843 | claim payments are probable and the costs can be reasonably estimated. Such liabilities are based on our best estimate of the undiscounted future costs required to complete the remedial work. The recorded liabilities are adjusted periodically as
009844 | remediation efforts progress or as additional technical, regulatory or legal information becomes available. Given the uncertainties regarding the status of laws, regulations, enforcement policies, the impact of other potentially responsible parties,
009845 | technology and information related to individual sites, we do not believe it is possible to develop an estimate of the range of reasonably possible environmental loss in excess of our recorded liabilities. We expect to fund expenditures for these
009846 | matters from operating cash flow. The timing of cash expenditures depends on a number of factors, including the timing of remedial investigations and feasibility studies, the timing of litigation and settlements of remediation liability, personal
009847 | injury and property damage claims, regulatory approval of cleanup projects, remedial techniques to be utilized and agreements with other parties.
009848 | F-46
009849 | Table of Contents
009850 | HOMES BUSINESS OF HONEYWELL INTERNATIONAL INC.
009851 | NOTES TO COMBINED INTERIM FINANCIAL STATEMENTS (Continued)
009852 | (Unaudited)
009853 | We accrue costs related to environmental matters when it is probable that we have incurred a
009854 | liability related to a contaminated site and the amount can be reasonably estimated. Environmental-related expenses are presented within Cost of goods sold for operating sites and Other expense for
009855 | non-operating sites in the Combined Interim Statements of Operations.

009889 | Other Matters
009890 | We are subject to other lawsuits, investigations and disputes arising out of the conduct of our business, including matters relating to
009891 | commercial transactions, government contracts, product liability, prior acquisitions and divestitures, employee benefit plans, intellectual property, and environmental, health and safety matters. We recognize a liability for any contingency that is
009892 | probable of occurrence and reasonably estimable. We continually assess the likelihood of adverse judgments of outcomes in these matters, as well as potential ranges of possible losses (taking into consideration any insurance recoveries), based on a
009893 | careful analysis of each matter with the assistance of outside legal counsel and, if applicable, other experts. To date, no such matters are material to the Combined Interim Statements of Operations.

009899 | Warranties and Guarantees

009901 | warranties and performance guarantees based on contract terms and historical experience at the time of sale. Adjustments to initial obligations for warranties and guarantees are made as changes to the obligations become reasonably estimable.

009917 | Note 12. Segment Financial Data
009918 | We globally manage our business operations through two reportable operating segments, Products and Distribution:
009919 | Products—Our Products business is a leading global provider of residential security and intrusion products, consumer thermostats,
009920 | consumer HVAC and consumer awareness systems, residential thermal solutions and residential water controls that allow owners of homes to stay connected and in control of their comfort, security and energy use.
009921 | Distribution—Our Distribution business is a leading global distributor of security and low voltage fire protection products.
009922 | Segment information is consistent with how management reviews the businesses, makes investing and resource allocation decisions and assesses
009923 | operating performance. Homes’ CODM evaluates segment performance based on segment profit. Segment profit is measured as segment income (loss) before taxes excluding, pension expense, repositioning and other charges, other expense, and interest
009924 | and other charges, net.
